6 Macro overview – at a glance

Rajat Bhattacharya 
Senior Investment Strategist

### Page 11

Global Market Outlook 11 
ECB to hike once more: Euro area activity has proved more 
resilient than expected. The consensus 2026 growth estimate 
has been revised up to 0.9%, from 0.5% as recently as July, 
with ECB staff projections upgraded to  1.4% for 2027 and 
1.5% for 2028. Manufacturing PMIs have improved to 52.7 
regionally and services PMI to 53.0, helped by German fiscal 
delivery and falling household savings rates. A softer Q3 looks 
weather-driven, due to heatwaves and low Rhine water levels, 
though higher energy prices and real yields still cap demand. 
Headline inflation hit a near three -year high of 3.3% y/y in 
August and may peak around 3.4 -3.6% in Q4 2026 before 
easing through 2027. Underlying pressures look calmer: core 
inflation drifted down to 2.4%, services eased, and negotiated 
wage growth slowed to 2.4% y/y in Q2, down from a peak of 
5.6% in 2024 . Long -term inflation expectations remain 
anchored with no evidence of second -round effects. Inflation 
risks are two -sided – oil above USD 100/bbl and gas above 
EUR 70/MWh are fuelling near -term inflation, which in turn 
should hurt demand, driving inflation below 2% by H2 2027. 
The ECB raised its deposit rate to 2.50% in September, the 
top of neutral estimates. We see scope for one more rate hike 
to 2.75% by year -end before a pause in H1 2027. As such 
pricing of 95bps more tightening toward an above-3% terminal 
rate appears too hawkish. We expect easing of energy-driven 
price pressures to allow for ECB rate cuts by Q3 2027. 
China to boost fiscal spending to support growth. China 
is entering a stabilisation phase in which production is firming, 
but the recovery remains narrow. The official manufacturing 
PMI rose to 49.8 in August from 49.2, with new orders, export 
orders and production back in expansionary territory, while 
export growth accelerated to 25.0% y/y. Momentum, however, 
is concentrated in high -tech and export manufacturing: retail 
sales, fixed-asset investment and credit growth remain soft, 
and the property downturn has yet to bottom. Full -year 2026 
growth should land near 4.5 –4.6%, broadly in line with the 
official 4.5–5% target, as domestic demand stays weak. 
Reflation is under way, but shallow and cost-driven rather than 
demand-led. Headline consumer inflation edged up to 0.8% 
y/y in August and producer inflation to 3.8%, lifted by higher 
commodity and energy prices and by official efforts to curb 
excessive price-cutting. Full-year inflation is expected around 
0.8–1.0%, against roughly zero in 2025, with producer 
inflation swinging from −2.6% to about +2.5%. The persistent 
supply-demand imbalance should cap the reflation impulse. 
Policy settings remain supportive, but with no rush to 
stimulate. Fiscal policy takes the primary role via faster 
spending and utilisation of bond proceeds, with broad fiscal 
spending potentially accelerating in H2 versus a sharp 
contraction in H1, and the augmented fiscal deficit widening 
modestly. Monetary policy plays a complementary role: a 
25bps cut in bank reserve requirements is likely, while the 
policy rate should stay unchanged. Slow public financing 
raises the likelihood of further policy fine-tuning this autumn. 
BoJ to hike once a quarter until Q2 2027. Japan's economy 
is proving more resilient than expected. The consensus 2026 
growth estimate has been revised up over the past month to 
0.8% from 0.6%. Exports rose 19% y/y in August, a twelfth 
consecutive y/y growth, while core machinery and machine 
tool orders indicate higher borrowing costs have yet to dent 
corporate investment appetite. Real wages climbed 2.4% y/y 
in July, the strongest since May 2021. The weak link is the 
household sector: spending continued to contract y/y, leaving 
an unusual two -speed economy in which external and 
corporate demand carry growth while domestic demand lags. 
Underlying inflation sits close to the 2% target, with pressure 
building upstream rather than from demand. Energy and 
commodity prices have risen sharply and earlier yen 
weakness has amplified import costs, with producer inflation 
running well ahead of CPI . The 10-year breakeven inflation 
has risen close to May’s record high of 2.3% amid growing risk 
of pass-through from producer to consumer prices.  
Bank of Japan Governor Ueda, after raising rates by 25bps to 
1.25% in September, alluded to rising inflation pressures, 
stating the policy environment has shifted to a “new phase”. 
He added that the BoJ must now act to prevent inflation from 
overshooting, rather than simply trying to reach the 2% target. 
September’s BoJ decision came in a 7-2 split vote, with two 
members nominated by Prime Minister Takaichi calling for a 
hold. However, Ueda’s post -decision comments lead us to 
believe that the BoJ board will be comfortable with a 25bps 
rate hike every quarter until Q2 2027, taking the rate to 2%.

### Page 12

Global Market Outlook 12

Our view

Foundation: Fixed income assets are a  core holding (Neutral) in our portfolios. Within the asset class, we maintain an 
Overweight stance on EM USD government bonds and an Underweight stance on DM government bonds.  
The Fed’s decision to hike rates in the past week confirms our expectations of a central bank willing to establish its independence 
against political pressure. The Fed's moderately hawkish pivot is helping restore its inflation-fighting credentials. We expect Fed 
to hike twice, one by year-end and another hike in H1 2027 to  bring the Fed funds rate to 4.5%. The price reaction in the US 
government bond yield curve le ads us to believe the short -to-middle part of the curve ha s factored in most of the rate 
expectations. We now prefer to add positions in the 3-7-year bond maturity bucket. As oil price moderates in H2 2027, we see 
room for the Fed to contemplate rate cuts. That would also enable 10-year yields to come down, alongside the front end of the 
curve. Meanwhile, solid corporate fundamentals and supportive technicals should keep yield premiums tight, even if the scope 
for further compression is limited. In EM, we continue to favour EM USD over local currency (LCY) government bonds. 
Opportunistic ideas: We are bullish on European bank additional tier-1 (AT1) bonds (contingent convertibles [CoCos1]; FX-
hedged), US TIPS, AAA-rated CLOs, US utilities corporate hybrids, broad US HY and AUD corporate bonds.

DM rates – Underweight 
We are Underweight DM government bonds. In the US, the 
Fed's policy pivot this month followed Chair Warsh's hawkish 
Jackson Hole speech and recent economic data prints, 
including a pick-up in job creation and inflation over the summer 
and a sharp rebound in oil prices due to the re-escalation of the 
Middle East conflict. Given the unanimous Fed decision to hike 
rates this week, we expect two more hikes by June 2027 on the 
back of robust growth amid strong AI investments and a 
balanced labour market, keeping inflation elevated.  
Over the next few months, we expect the 2-, 10- and 30-year 
US government bond yields to remain rangebound, with the 
10-year yield trading in the 5.00-5.25% range. The reasons 
for this include: 1) oil prices staying elevated and potentially 
rising further due to the fluid geopolitical situation in the Middle 
East; 2) AI capex plans showing no signs of slowing down, 
which continues to drive above -trend growth in the economy 
and AI -related heavy bond issuance. While short -covering 
trades could move bond yields lower temporarily as net-short 
positions in US government bond futures have reached 
extreme levels, we think such downshifts would be short-lived. 
By H2 2027, we expect oil prices to moderate and the 
inflationary effects from tariffs to fade, allowing the Fed to 
contemplate rate cuts. US 30 -year yields, on the other hand, 
are expected to remain elevated as the US fiscal deficit 
remains substantial next year. In addition, we do not think the 
so-called ‘Bessent put’  - expanding purchases of long -dated 
bonds, while skewing new issuance towards shorter 
maturities - will be effective as this does not address the 
fundamental issues  of rising debt levels . These factors are 
likely to keep the 'term premium' elevated. 
In the Euro area and Japan, the ECB and BoJ also hiked rates 
this month. It is the first time on record all three - Fed, ECB 
and BoJ - have hiked in the same month, as they respond 
primarily to a resurgence in energy -driven inflation amid tight 
job markets. BoJ is expected to continue hiking once every 
quarter till mid -2027 as we believe the bank  is behind the 
curve and will need to speed up the pace of rate hikes.

### Page 13

Global Market Outlook 13 
DM corporates – Core holding  
We maintain a Neutral allocation to both DM IG and DM HY 
corporate bonds, with a tactical bias to add exposure on 
episodic weakness, as carry continues to dominate total 
returns across developed market credit. Yield premiums have 
bounced but remain close to historical lows, while yields are 
still attractive, reinforcing a carry-led stance. This tight spread 
regime is anchored by robust issuer fundamentals, strong 
profitability, and resilient balance sheets, despite a visible 
moderation in rating upgrades over the past quarter.  
Fig. 16 AI issuance challenges IG technical backdrop 
AI issuance (total & share of total), US IG gross issuance

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered 
Technical supply dynamics are reshaping sector dispersion, 
particularly in the IG space, where heavy issuance related to 
AI continues to weigh on the long end of the curve. AI -driven 
supply is approaching USD 220bn for 2026  in US IG  and 
accounting for an increasingly significant share of gross 
issuance and crowding out other long-dated maturity bonds. 
We maintain a preference for selectivity at the long end, 
favouring the front to belly of the curve ( 3-7 years maturity) 
where risk -adjusted carry remains most durable. Issuance -
driven concessions in sectors with strong capital expenditure 
cycles (notably technology, capital goods, and basic 
industries) present opportunities for investors who can 
stomach near term volatility, especially when fund flows have 
remained robust: so far this year, high grade funds have seen 
cumulative inflows of ~8% of AUM. 
Fig. 17 DM IG spreads have bounced lately, but remain 
near historical lows 
Bloomberg Global Aggregate Corp Index, option-adjusted 
spread (OAS)

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered 
As an income proposition, DM HY continues to be well 
supported, with all-in yields in the mid -7% range and default 
rate still below trend for US HY in 2026. The shorter duration 
profile of HY, combined with its lower sensitivity to term 
premium and rate volatility, makes it an attractive sleeve in the 
current phase of the cycle. Despite experiencing net outflow 
YTD, HY demand has recovered from a net outflow position 
in the past 1-2 months, underscoring broad investor appetite 
for carry strategies even as spreads remain tight. We remain 
attentive to evolving rating migration trends and sector -
specific supply pressures, particularly as upgrades slow and 
new issuance clusters around key theme s. However, we  
continue to see HY bonds as core holding allocations driven 
by healthy issuer fundamentals and stable credit metrics. 
Fig. 18 DM HY spreads are near historical lows as well 
Bloomberg Global HY Aggregate Corporate Index, OAS

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered 
EM government bonds – Overweight USD, 
core holding for local currency (LCY) bonds 
We re tain an  Overweight position in EM USD government 
bonds and retain EM LCY government bonds as a Core 
holding, reflecting a balanced perspective across both 
regional performance and currency dynamics. In EM USD 
sovereign debt, periods of spread widening have been brief  
and shallow, with attractive carry drawing sustained demand 
amid resilient global growth and stable external conditions. 
Issuance patterns remain front -loaded across many regions, 
reinforcing supportive technicals; relatively light investor 
positioning con tributes to contained risk of unwinding and 
limits market impact during episodes of moderate outflow. 
Regional nuances are increasingly notable. Latin America’s 
sovereign debt has tended to outperform, supported by more 
proactive monetary policy responses and firmer external 
balances. In Asia, higher -rated issuers offer valuable buffer 
during bouts of glob al rate volatility, while select frontier 
exposures within Africa and EMEA remain more sensitive to 
liquidity shifts and changes in risk appetite. Index composition 
also shapes resilience: the EM USD benchmark is less 
exposed to oil price shocks as non -oil importers represent a 
large share of exposure compared to the LCY universe. These 
features are particularly important as spreads across EM USD 
sovereigns hover near historically tight levels, leaving limited 
room for error. We expect returns to be primarily driven by 
carry rather than further spread compression.

### Page 15

Global Market Outlook 15

Our view

We are Overweight global equities as we expect solid earnings growth to drive the market higher . The wave of central 
bank rate hikes in September represent a near-term headwind to equity valuations, but these have been largely priced into 
markets by the rise in bond yields year-to-date. We expect earnings growth to offset valuation headwinds. We are Overweight 
US and AxJ equities, where AI adoption continues to drive growth in the technology and adjacent sectors. 
Within AxJ, we remain Overweight Taiwan, with good visibility on AI-driven growth. We lower China to a Core holding, 
as the valuation re-rating potential appears delayed. Within AxJ, we maintain core allocations to South Korea and India, and we 
are Underweight on ASEAN equities. 
We maintain a Core allocation to Japan, as nominal growth supports broad earnings revision, and a Core allocation to Europe 
ex-UK, which is enjoying an earnings inflexion. We remain Underweight UK equities, which lacks exposure to growth sectors.

### Page 16

Global Market Outlook 16

Expansion at a solid pace  
With economic activity expanding at a solid pace , we expect 
earnings growth to fundamentally drive global equities higher. 
Heading into 2027, rising AI adoption continues to drive 
strong growth  in the technology sector. Recent debates 
about regulating the pace of model developments could 
influence the mix of AI spending, with differing impacts on 
various parts of the AI value chain. However, rising adoption 
and growth in AI use cases should support massive growth in 
capex, benefitting the semiconductor industry, cloud service 
providers and internet platforms. The capex benefits also 
spillover to other industries providing the power infrastructure, 
materials and financing of the investmen ts. The risks are a 
slowdown in AI capex or net job losses from AI disruption, but 
we do not see these having a significant impact yet. 
Rate hikes  from central banks represent a headwind for 
equity valuations, although this has been largely priced in by 
bond markets, given the rise in yields we have seen this year. 
Further rate hikes are being priced by the market. However, 
we expect these headwinds to be offset by solid earnings 
growth in global equities: at 34% in 2026 and 16% in 2027.  
Fig. 21 Global equities continue to be supported by 
strong earnings growth, led by the technology sector  
Earnings growth by sectors for MSCI AC World index

Source: Company reports, Standard Chartered  
US equities – Overweight  
US equity valuations have softened this year  as strong 
earnings growth have outpaced the rise in the market. The 
12m forward P/E of around 20x remains at a premium to other 
markets but justified, in our view, by the high returns on equity, 
differences in sector composition and superior growth outlook. 
In the run-up to US mid-term elections in November, we would 
not be surprised to see higher volatility, though we would use 
any undue corrections as opportunities to add exposure. 
AxJ equities – Overweight 
AxJ enjoys the strongest earnings growth among major 
markets, as its technology sector remains a key 
beneficiary of the AI capex cycle. AxJ also has the lowest 
P/E valuation, given the earnings surge ha s not yet been  
reflected in the market. Within AxJ, we are Overweight 
Taiwan, which benefits from clear visibility on AI -driven 
growth and dominance in manufacturing advanced AI chips. 
We lower China to a Core holding as the valuation re -
rating potential now appears delayed. Share placements for 
AI spending will likely continue, diluting earnings. Regulatory 
concerns still linger from US restrictions on AI chips and 
distillation accusations, even as the Trump-Xi summit signals 
a mutual desire for steady ties. Looming offshore trust taxes 
may lead to further sell down pressures , while domestic 
demand remains weak. However, valuation s remain 
inexpensive with rapid tech innovation and AI -localisation 
investments. Policy stimulus could provide upside. 
South Korea equities remain a Core holding. We are 
positive on the long-term agreements giving greater earnings 
visibility on memory chip s. Positioning is also cleaner, with 
reduced foreign investor exposure and declin ing leveraged 
ETFs. India remains a Core holding amid positive earnings 
revision and strong domestic demand , alongside  macro 
headwinds from RBI rate hikes. We remain Underweight on 
ASEAN, as oil shocks and a supported USD could weigh on 
the region, although Singapore offers defensive growth. 
Fig. 22 Global equity valuations have declined this year, 
with market gains led by earnings growth 
Composition of returns YTD for regional MSCI equity indices

Source: FactSet, Standard Chartered. Min-Max over last 10 years 
Japan equities – Core allocation 
Rising nominal economic growth is supporting solid 
broad-based earnings revisions , with rising rates 
supportive of bank earnings. The fiscal support for strategic 
industries is also pro-growth, offsetting energy shocks.  
Europe ex-UK equities – Core allocation  
Europe ex-UK is seeing an earnings inflection  after three 
stagnant years, with rising rates supportive of banks’ 
earnings. Fiscal expansion is benefitting domestic demand 
and the industrial sector, although earnings growth in the 
region still lags that of the US and AxJ.  
UK equities – Underweight  
The UK lacks exposure to growth sectors, with relatively 
muted earnings growth. We expect this to weigh on the UK , 
making it lag the performance of other markets.

Add Global Semiconductors, remove HSTECH 
We initiate d an Opportunistic Idea on Global 
Semiconductors on 9 Sep t 2026. We expect sustained AI 
investments and rising AI adoption to support earnings 
growth, with demand widening beyond processors to memory, 
networking, and advanced manufacturing equipment . 
Valuations have also eased since the middle of the year. 
We remove Hang Seng Technology (31 Oct 2024 to 24 Sep 
2026: -1.2%) amid mixed earnings trends, while AI 
monetisation has yet to keep pace with rising investments. 
Widening US-China tech restrictions adds to the uncertainty, 
and we see better risk-adjusted opportunities elsewhere.

Ongoing Opportunistic Ideas 
Euro area banks: A higher-for-longer rates backdrop should 
extend the earnings upcycle, with further ECB tightening  
expected. Corporate loan demand is showing recovery, while 
non-interest income  is growing . Strong capital generation 
supports higher dividends and continued share buybacks. 
Global Power & Electrification: Rising AI and data centre 
power demand should drive a multi -year grid and 
electrification investment cycle. Power availability and grid 
capacity are emerging bottlenecks to the AI infra buildout. 
Addressing these constraints requires capex and time, which 
supports strong earnings visibility for grid equipment makers. 
Japan Banks : BoJ policy normali sation drives net interest 
margin upside, while Japan’s reflation cycle supports nominal 
loan demand, corporate capex and wealth activity. 
Governance reform is a re-rating driver  independent of the 
rate cycle. This includes recycling legacy holdings towards 
growth, buybacks and dividends.  
MSCI Taiwan: Taiwan’s foundry leadership makes it a critical 
supplier to global tech firms, while its broadening AI hardware 
supply chain is driving strong revenue growth.  
Global High Dividend  payers: Provides a higher -income 
equity allocation with historically lower volatility than the MSCI 
ACWI and modest downside cushioning on balance.  
US Communication Services: We remain constructive and 
expect further upward earnings revisions, driven by improving 
digital ad-spending and AI monetisation. 
Sector views: Continue to broaden & diversify 
We upgrade US Materials to Overweight as robust capex 
drives demand for industrial metals and construction 
materials, while we remain bullish on gold . US Technology 
and Communication Services remain the primary growth 
engines, supported by structural AI tailwinds. We remain 
Overweight in Financials across the US, Europe ex -UK, and 
Japan, underpinned by earnings stability  and elevated rates 
supporting interest income. This is further supported by a US 
capital markets recovery  and European balance -sheet 
strength. We remain Overweight in Europe ex-UK Industrials, 
reflecting EU fiscal stimulus and Germany's infrastructure 
acceleration legislation. 
In China, we broaden our Overweight exposure by upgrading 
three sectors: Materials (benefiting from structurally elevated 
commodity prices), Healthcare (biotech innovation and a robust 
pipeline sustaining growth), and Financials (easing margin 
pressure, fee recovery, and capital market reform). Our existing 
Overweight positio ns in Technology and Communication 
Services maintain exposure to China's AI build-out, with fiscal 
deployment into high-tech infrastructure serving as a near-term 
catalyst. On the other hand, we downgrade Utilities to 
Underweight on capped margins and retain our Underweight 
views on Real Estate and Consumer Staples.

Our view

• We maintain our Overweight on gold, and our 3 - and 12-month price targets at USD 4,750/oz and 5,000/oz, respectively. 
• We raise our 3-month West Texas Intermediate (WTI) oil forecast to USD 100/bbl and 12-month target to USD 80/bbl.

Gold outlook:  Gold has remained resilient despite a more 
hawkish Fed and higher real yields, suggesting much of the 
tightening is already reflected in prices. With markets 
discounting an aggressive Fed path, we see an increasingly 
asymmetric risk -reward: limited downsid e risk from further 
rate repricing, but meaningful upside should Fed rate 
expectations shift towards being less restrictive. Official -
sector demand has slowed from recent years but has become 
broader based, while investor positioning remains well below 
levels seen at the start of the 2022 hiking cycle. 
Oil outlook: Oil prices have risen sharply as recent attacks 
cast greater uncertainty over Saudi export s, even as Gulf 
crude flows overall remain resilient. Other regional producers 
have helped sustain exports, containing the immediate supply 
impact. However, adequate US commercial crude stocks 
mask thinner buffers elsewhere, with strategic reserves and 
fuel inventories low. This leaves the m arket more vulnerable 
to a prolonged disruption, supporting a higher geopolitical risk 
premium and an upward revision to our outlook.

15 Gold, crude oil – at a glance

Anthony Naab, CFA 
Investment Strategist

### Page 19

Global Market Outlook 19

USD view

Further Fed tightening supports the USD near term. We expect the USD to remain supported, with our 3-month DXY forecast 
at 100.2. Resilient US payrolls for August alongside sticky energy and IT price -driven inflation reinforce the Fed's focus on 
containing inflation. Following its 25bp hike to 3.75–4.00%, we expect another hike by end-2026 and potentially another one in 
early H1 2027. Further tightening should keep US front -end yields elevated and support the USD. However, money markets 
already price a more aggressive Fed hiking path than our expectations, limiting the scope for further USD gains and leaving the 
USD vulnerable if incoming data weaken the case for additional hikes. 
Fading inflation and narrowing rate advantage point to a softer USD in the medium term. We expect the USD index (DXY) 
to decline gradually towards 98 over 12 months. Inflation pressures are expected to moderate  as the impact of oil prices and 
tariffs fade, reducing the need for continued Fed tightening. We forecast that the Fed funds rate may peak in H1 2027  in the 
current mini-rate hike cycle and then gradually decline thereafter as inflation moves closer towards target , supporting our DXY 
call. Further ECB and BoJ tightening should also progressively narrow the USD's relative rate advantage, supporting gradual 
USD moderation. Resilient US growth and elevated government bond yields should, however, limit the pace of depreciation.

### Page 21

Global Market Outlook 21

Summary

Our stock-bond model (3-6 months) reduced its Overweight allocation to global equities to +1 from +3 (maximum +5) 
in September. The downgrade was driven primarily by a weaker valuation signal as valuation score fell to -2 for both Developed 
Market and Asian equities. Despite some moderation, fundamentals remain strongly supportive of equities. Positive factors 
include resilient net earnings upgrades, a positive economic surprise index, and PMI new orders remaining above 50, signalling 
continued expansion in industrial activity. Market technicals weakened but continued to support equities overall. Our market 
breadth indicator shows that 72% of equity markets remain above their 200 -day moving averages. Stock net advances are 
positive and not at levels that would typically signal an imminent market reversal. The stock-bond model continues to outperform 
strongly, generating 4.2% alpha YTD relative to the 60/40 equity-bond benchmark. 
Our 1–3 month equity market regime models continue to signal low bear market risk, with probabilities of just 0.7% for 
S&P 500 and 5.0% for MSCI AC World  indices. The low readings continued to be driven by subdued option -market implied 
volatility. At 14 – 15%, the VIX and market cap -weighted one-month implied volatility measures for Europe and US remain 
comfortably below the 20% threshold at which bear market risk tends to rise sharply. While momentum indicators have softened, 
they point to a typical market consolidation rather than signs of meaningful market stress.  
Our positioning indicators are not signalling any reversal risk. Previous long positioning in DXY has fully unwound and our 
market diversity indicator for MSCI Singapore has also normalized after the index corrected from its September peak.

15 Quant perspective: Reducing Equity 
Overweight as Valuation Weakened

Francis Lim Senior Quantitative Strategist Maggie, Au Yeung Quantitative Analyst

### Page 22

Global Market Outlook 22 
Fig. 36 Long- and short-term quantitative models remain bullish on risk assets 
Long-term models below have a typical time horizon of 3-6 months, while short-term models have a 1-3-month horizon

Long-term Stock or bond US recession model 
Current view Reduce equity Overweight 20% probability within the next 6 months 
What factors 
is this view 
based on? 
• Fundamental: +2. 3-month change in PMI new 
orders indicate a slower pace in the expansion of 
industrial activity. All other factors such as net 
earnings upgrades, economic surprise index and 
risk sentiment remain positive.  
• Valuation: -2. Both DM and Asian equities are 
expensive as their valuation scores exceeded 1 
standard deviation of historical average.  
• Technical: +1. Net-advances in global stocks are 
positive but not extreme. Market breadth declined 
as equity markets above their 200-day moving 
averages fell from 85% to 72%, but it remains 
above the model’s 60% threshold. 
Estimated probability hovers around 20 – 21% 
• Conference Board consumer confidence 
remains weak at -8.6% y/y. 
• PMI new orders-to-inventory ratio is at 1.1x, 
below its historical average of 1.2x.  
• Conference Board leading indicators 
contracted by -1.1% y/y.  
• Fed funds rate-to-natural rate ratio is still high 
at 1.6x, reflecting a restrictive monetary policy 
compared to historical average of 1.3x. 
Key model 
factors 
• Economic activity, macro risk and surprise indices, 
corporate earnings, forward P/E ratio and technical 
factors. 
• Conference Board leading indicator, Consumer 
Confidence Index, ISM New Orders and Fed 
funds rate. 
How does it 
work? 
• A monthly scorecard of -5 to 5 based on 
fundamentals, valuations and market breadth 
factors to indicate the relative preference for bonds 
and equities. A positive score favours equities and 
vice versa. 
• A probability model based on regression. Long-
term statistical tests are conducted to identify 
the four key economic factors above.

Short-term Technical analysis Investor positioning 
Current views Bullish S&P 500 & MSCI AC World indices None flagged 
What factors 
is this view 
based on? 
• Bullish S&P500 and MSCI AC World. The 
probabilities of bear market sit at just 0.7% and 
5.0%, respectively, driven by subdued options-
market implied volatility. Momentum across both 
indices have weakened but they point to a typical 
market consolidation rather than signs of meaningful  
market stress.  
• Previous long positioning in the USD index 
(DXY) has fully unwound after DXY fell 
steeply form its peak in late July. The renewed 
strength in DXY since the Fed’s 16 Sept rate 
hike has started from neutral positioning.  
• Market diversity indicator for MSCI 
Singapore has normalised after the index 
corrected from its September peak. 
Key model 
factors 
• Market factors: Momentum, volatility, interest rate 
differentials, relative returns, inflation swap rates, 
economic surprises, etc. 
• Price action: Overbought conditions occur 
when prices rise sharply; oversold conditions 
happen when prices fall rapidly in a short time.  
How does it 
work? 
• Scanning through 7,000+ factors, the framework 
uses Machine Learning to forecast market regimes 
or future trends based on identified market drivers. 
• A market indicator based on fractal analysis 
that provides a timely indication of investor 
positioning based on price actions.

Time is your most precious commodity – be sure to spend it wisely 
Time is undoubtedly valuable. The days may seem long, but the years are short. So, make the choice 
to spend your time wisely. Whether you’re setting out on your investment journey, navigating the 
intricacies of mid -life wealth planning or fortifying assets  for your golden years, invest time today to 
ensure your wealth strategy is aligned to what’s right for you – Today, Tomorrow and Forever. 
Setting aside the time now to review your plan will pay dividends in the future. Markets have moved. 
Your portfolio’s current asset allocation may no longer be optimally positioned to maximise the 
opportunities ahead. Ask yourself the following: Am I holding too much cash? Am I sufficiently allocating 
to growth assets for the long term? Is my portfolio diversified? Am I capturing the best opportunities? 
And most importantly, is my wealth working hard for me so that I don’t have to? 
Use our SC Wealth Select framework and advisory specialists to help guide you through this process.

Purpose 
Today,  
Tomorrow,  
Forever 
Our approach to helping you grow and manage 
your wealth starts with you. We use a goals -
aware approach to understand your vision of 
Today, Tomorrow and Forever for yourself, your 
family and beyond, and then design portfolios to 
meet your various needs.  
Using our ‘Today, Tomorrow and Forever’ 
approach, we ensure your wealth needs for the 
near term (Today) are met, while ensuring your 
wealth needs for the decades ahead (Tomorrow 
and Forever) are also planned for.  
Your vision of ‘Today, Tomorrow and Forever’ is 
unique to you. Our specialists partner with you to 
build well -diversified, long -term Foundation 
portfolios, aligned to your Today, Tomorrow and 
Forever needs. Opportunistic ideas are added to 
capture short -term opportunities, a nd sufficient 
protection is included to address the objectives of 
you and your family. 
Today, Tomorrow and Forever 
Approach 
Planning for Today 
Requires ensuring liquidity and income 
flows take centre stage 
Securing Tomorrow 
Entails a well-diversified investment and 
protection portfolio with a focus on growth, 
ensuring inflation is accounted for and risks 
are mitigated 
Building for Forever 
Involves greater focus on long-term returns 
given the time horizon of your portfolio can 
be measured in decades, and might also 
include business interests, real estate, 
collectibles or charitable funds

SC Wealth Select  
 Managing your wealth through the decades – Today, Tomorrow and Forever

### Page 28

Global Market Outlook 28

Principles 
that stand the 
test of time 
Adhering to time-tested principles, to ensure your 
investment decisions remain robust and 
consistently applied, is paramount to your 
success Today, Tomorrow and Forever. We use 
five Wealth Principles to guide and guardrail your 
wealth decisions.

Discipline – ensure consistency and 
prudence over your emotions 
• Reacting to emotions, such as optimism and 
fear, can lead to poor investment decisions at 
the worst times 
• Have a plan and stick to it – this helps you to 
stay focused on the bigger picture

Diversification – simply put, don’t put 
all your eggs in one basket 
• Reduce risk by holding a variety of financial 
assets. Multi -asset diversification in your 
Foundation portfolio is important 
• As a guide, make sure your portfolio contains 
a variety of asset classes and investments 
that have low correlation with one another

Time in the Market – a more robust 
strategy than timing the market

• Predicting market selloffs is challenging, and 
timing your exit and re-entry is difficult 
• Missing out on the best performing days of a 
market can have a significantly detrimental 
impact on your portfolio 
• ‘Time in the market’ and buying the market 
with a longer -term view provide more 
consistent returns that can ride out bumps 
along the way

Risk and Return – make sure the risk 
is worth the return 
• To achieve higher investment returns, you will 
likely have to accept a greater level of risk in 
your portfolio 
• Therefore, it’s important to understand the 
risks and manage these on an ongoing basis

Protection – don’t let the unexpected 
catch you unprepared 
• Even though you may feel healthy, or 
financially stable now, protection offers the 
ability to overcome times of financial 
uncertainty and mitigate the long-term impact 
of unforeseen events on your wealth 
• A good protection plan not only safeguards 
your wealth today, but also considers the 
value of your future earnings over your 
lifetime, in today’s terms

Advisory 
Process 
Following a 
holistic 
approach to 
managing  
your wealth 
We follow a rigorous process to ensure your 
needs and objectives are well -understood, and 
your portfolio is well-aligned and manage s to  
deliver on these objectives. 
However, markets constantly evolve and your 
needs change. Hence, we encourage you to 
undertake regular portfolio reviews to ensure your 
portfolio remains aligned to your Today, 
Tomorrow and Forever objectives. This proactive 
approach includes strategic reb alancing based 
on insights from our Chief Investment Office.

## Document 343: ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD_ November 2-6

Source PDF: all report pdf/ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD_ November 2-6.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-10-31
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD: November 2-6
Date:    3 hours ago
ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD: November 2-6

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD:
November 2-6
By Ed Yardeni & Toby Hearst • 31 Oct 2026
View in browser

### Page 2

Photo by Monica Sauro / Unsplash

### Page 3

The S&P 500 fell 0.6% last week, and the Nasdaq dropped 2.0%, both
posting their third straight week of losses, as disappointing tech earnings
revived concerns about AI-related spending.
The FOMC held the federal funds rate (FFR) at 3.75%-4.00% on
Wednesday in a 10-2 vote, with the dissenters favoring a 25bps hike. We
believe the committee needs another 50bps of increases to get back to a
neutral FFR that is neither accommodative nor restrictive.

October’s employment report (Fri) caps a jam-packed week of labor
market data, including JOLTS (Tue), ADP (Wed), and Challenger and
jobless claims (Thu). ISM’s M-PMI (Mon) and NM-PMI (Wed) are also
due. Six Fed officials are scheduled to speak. Tuesday’s midterm
elections should be interesting. Rate decisions from the Reserve Bank of
Australia and the Bank of England and earnings reports from Apple,
Palantir, AMD, and Qualcomm round out the week's calendar. Here’s

more:
(1) Employment. October’s employment report is the economic headliner.
Payrolls rose 29,000 in September, lowering the three-month average to
50,700 (chart). The unemployment rate rose to 4.2% from 4.1%, while
average hourly earnings increased just 0.1% m/m. We expect a stronger
payroll gain than the consensus, of approximately 55,000.
Challenger’s October layoff announcements (Thu) follow September’s
count of 43,300. Technology led September’s announced cuts, at 10,800.

Initial unemployment claims (Thu) remain low, with a four-week average of

### Page 4

197,800 for the week ended October 24 (chart). We expect more of the
same for both labor market indicators this week.
The four-week moving average of ADP's employment measure peaked at
26,000 in late September before easing to 16,750 in early October (chart).
September’s monthly ADP report showed a 90,000 gain in private
payrolls, nearly double the Bureau of Labor Statistics’ 46,000.
September’s JOLTS report (Tue) follows August’s 7.1 million job openings,

down from 7.3 million in July. The share of consumers saying jobs are
“plentiful” was 26.7% in October, little changed from 27.0% in August
(chart).

### Page 5

(2) PMIs. ISM’s M-PMI (Mon) follows September’s 54.5 reading, with the
prices-paid index at 77.9. The NM-PMI (Wed) was 54.9 (chart). S&P
Global’s flash M-PMI edged up to 56.0 in October from September’s 55.9,
a 53-month high, while the flash NM-PMI eased to 57.9 from 58.8. Both
still point to solid ISM readings.
S&P 500 forward earnings rose 38.8% y/y as of October 26, up from
37.2% in September, suggesting more upside for the M-PMI (chart).

### Page 6

(3) Earnings. The earnings reporting season continues this week, with
31% of the S&P 500 companies set to report. Apple, Palantir, AMD, Arista
Networks, Qualcomm, and Arm headline the list.
Analysts’ consensus estimates imply Q3-2026 operating EPS growth for
S&P 500 companies of 23.8% y/y, down slightly from 24.1% last week,
after Alphabet, Amazon, and Intel missed estimates (chart). Q4 growth
estimates held at 30.2%.
(4) Global. The Reserve Bank of Australia (Tue) is expected to hike its

cash rate target by 25bps for a second straight meeting, to 4.85% from
4.60% (chart). The markets are pricing in 88% odds of that result, so it’s
no sure thing. Last week, the Reserve Bank of New Zealand held its
official cash rate at 2.75% despite 95% odds of a hike. Both central banks
are often seen as bellwethers for the global rate cycle.

### Page 7

The Bank of England (Thu) looks set to raise its rate by 25bps to 4.00%,
with 98% odds priced into the markets. The markets see a 35% chance of
another hike in December.
The Eurozone’s flash CPI (Wed) is expected to rise in October from
September’s 3.8% y/y, with the core rate holding at 2.5%. Among the
major economies, Spain’s headline rate was the highest at 5.0% in
September and Germany’s the lowest at 3.3% (chart).

## Document 372: India_ Degrees of rupee defence (2026-10-16)

Source PDF: all report pdf/India_ Degrees of rupee defence (2026-10-16).pdf
Report source: DBS

### Page 1

TAKING STOCK

The Reserve Bank of India (RBI) has adopted a 
multi-layered defence strategy to support the 
rupee this year.

This has entailed a combination of outright 
intervention in spot/ forwards, regulatory 
action, liquidity steps, administrative measures 
and moves to curb import -related dollar 
demand.

The rupee has appreciated by 1.8% against the 
dollar in the past five days since recent 
measures took effect, paring YTD depreciation 
to 5.5% . High oil prices, weakness in capital 
inflows, a firm dollar , tariffs/ geopolitical 
worries, energy , and risk -off sentiments have 
kept the INR under pressure since last year.

India: Degrees of rupee defence

16 October 2026 
Radhika Rao, Senior Economist 
radhikarao@dbs.com

### Page 2

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            2

ECHOES OF 2013 WITH FEW 
DIFFERENCES

Comparisons with 2013 are apt, though 
the backdrop differs.  As for the 
catalysts, markets reacted to the US 
Fed’s taper announcement in 2013, 
which pushed US yields higher  and led 
global investors to reduce exposure to 
vulnerable EM economies. As a result, 
capital inflows weakened, exposing 
funding mismatch es for countries, 
including India, that were running wide 
current account deficits.

In 2026, markets face the spectre of 
elevated oil prices, geopolitical 
uncertainty, global competition for 
capital, a firm dollar ( DXY appreciated 
+3.8% YTD) , and US inflation/ deficits 
which have settled at levels much higher 
than pre -pandemic years. This has 
pushed up UST yields, and the US Fed 
kicked off its rate-hike cycle in Sep26.

Against this backdrop, the RBI is 
pursuing the same objective , i.e., to 
ensure rupee stability, but the nature of 
the battle differs. The defence strategy 
has shifted from ‘crisis management’ 
mode in 2013 , due to weaker domestic 
macroeconomic balances (a wide current 
account gap, high inflation, a lower 
reserve buffer, and wide fiscal deficits) , 
to a ‘terms-of-trade shock and impact of 
a firm dollar’ in 2026.

Flashpoints are  nonetheless similar, 
with reliance on portfolio financing , 
leaving the external account vulnerable 
to shifts in global risk sentiment. 
Portfolio outflows have emerged as the 
primary transmission channel through 
which external shocks have translated 
into INR depreciation pressure , 
compounded by high energy prices and a 
firm dollar.

FX PLAYBOOK

While the response framework is similar 
between 2013 and this year, the broad 
sequence and nature of macroprudential 
measures differ. This year’s multi-
layered rupee defence toolkit has 
included:

-Direct FX defence  by way of 
intervention in the spot and forwards 
market. As of Aug26, the net forward 
dollar liabilities ballooned to $200bn, 
rising by ~$63bn vs . the month before . 
Recent sell/buy FX swaps are expected 
to lower this stock in the coming months.

-Attracting dollar inflows  by way of 
special swap facilities for FCNR deposits, 
and offshore borrowings , apart from 
liberalising taxes around debt inflows,

### Page 3

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            3

amongst others. We discussed this here, 
here, here and here.

Taking a proactive stance, a portion of 
existing FX reserves could be earmarked 
against forward liabilities (from maturing 
FCNR deposits) , helping to mitigate 
concerns that deposit maturities or debt 
repayments could trigger a sharp
increase in dollar ⁠demand down the line.

-Domestic liquidity management. Bonds, 
to some degree, are paying the price for 
the authorities' efforts to defend the 
rupee against the additional headwind of 
weak global conditions.  INR liquidity 
surged on the back of the swap windows, 
prompting the RBI to undertake open 
market operations, VRRR auctions, sell -
buy swaps, among others. While these 
have been effective in absorbing 
balances, bond yields have drifted higher. 
10Y yields are at three-year highs and are 
expected to climb further  (DBSf: 7.40 -
7.50% by year-end).

-Moderate FX demand and speculation . 
Earlier in the year, the RBI had 
introduced change s to the banks’ net 
open position framework, and thereafter 
partially rolled back a few curbs. 
Prudential measures on FX exposures 
and hedging -related measures were 
announced in Oct26, which include d the 
introduction of a daily cash reserve with 
the RBI equal to 20% of the rupee 
equivalent of the notional value for INR -
linked derivative contracts exceeding 
$2mn used to hedge current account 
transactions, referred to as the Foreign 
Exchange Risk Reserve (FERR) . 
Subsequent clarifications provided 
operational details on where the FERR 
would be maintained and that it would be 
subject to audits.

Since 12 Oct, the RBI has been providing 
dollars via designated banks to the three 
state-owned oil -marketing companies 
(Indian Oil, BPCL, and HPCL) to meet the 
their daily dollar requirements, thereby 
diverting related spot-market demand.

On hedging-related matters, the limit for 
undertaking foreign exchange derivative 
transactions without proving an 
underlying commercial exposure was 
cut to $5mn from $100mn across all ADs 
and exchange -traded currency 
derivatives. Restrictions on Rebooking , 
i.e., a uthorized dealers are prohibited 
from allowing customers to rebook 
cancelled rupee -linked derivative 
contracts (though rollovers upon 
maturity remain permitted). Lastly, 
banks must obtain written confirmation 
from users that the same underlying 
exposure has not been hedged with 
another dealer.  Data from CCI , cited by

### Page 4

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            4

Bloomberg, pointed to importers’ 
forward dollar purchases rising 44% on 
the year to a monthly average of $64bn.

-Current account management . Efforts 
to contain structural FX outflows by way 
of higher duties and taxes on gold 
imports discourage non -essential 
purchases and production incentives to 
improve export competitiveness.

IMPACT

To assess whether the playbook has 
worked, we observe:

a) Rupee has enjoyed a brief respite. 
Despite oil prices at $105/bbl at the time 
of writing and elevated global yields, 
USDINR fell from near 96.80 to wards 
95.00, owing to measures announced in 
Oct26 following the central bank’s 25bp 
hike. Beyond temporary relief, global 
catalysts are likely to be back to the fore 
and weigh on the currency (see chart);

b) Sharp inflows under the swap 
facilities pushed the foreign reserves 
stock to a record high, providing 
policymakers sufficient buffer to 
navigate future shocks from a position of 
strength. A strong interventionist 
response has since led to a softening in 
the overall stock (see chart).

c) Macroprudential measures have led to 
distortions in other parts of the market . 
For instance, strong swap -related 
inflows had inorganically pushed 
onshore INR liquidity up sharply, 
necessitating absorption measures. 
Similarly, NOP restrictions and the more 
recent tighter requirements on hedging 
activity and counterparty transactions  
had some after -effects. Forward points 
have surged this week , with financial 
institutions pointing to relative illiquidity 
in the currency forwards market  on 
volumes and activity basis, and fewer 
avenues to deal with dollar supply.

### Page 5

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            5

d) Swap-related windows are a timely 
support for the country’s balance of 
payments position. From expectations of 
a third consecutive year of BOP deficit in 
FY27, the math has now swung to a 
potential surplus, even as current 
account worries remain. With energy 
prices settling north of $100/bbl, 
keeping the monthly trade deficit near 
$30bn, our FY27 current account deficit 
forecast of 1.1% of GDP faces  upside 
risks of around 0.3-0.5% of GDP. We note 
the s harp jump in ECB outflows in S ep, 
though see that as a temporary overshot 
of leverage-FCNR activity.

WHAT LIES AHEAD

Authorities are likely not done yet and 
remain ready to take further action, as 
required. RBI Governor Malhotra 
reiterated the authorities’ serious intent 
of stabilising the currency in a recent 
interview (Moneycontrol, 13 Oct), 
emphasising that “The RBI is determined 
to do whatever is necessary to ensure 
orderly movement in the foreign 
exchange market,”. He also highlighted 
that recent steps were not tantamount to 
capital control measures that were 
aimed at ensuring that forex markets 
serve genuine hedging needs rather than 
speculative bets.  Market participants 
opine that FX volumes and interest are 
likely to be subdued in a few pockets on 
the back of the recent measures, 
consequently stabilizing the rupee.

Unlike in 2013, when policy tightening 
was a part of the initial response, the 
2026 strategy has prioritised targeted 
FX and regulatory measures.  The 
sequencing suggests a deliberate effort 
to distinguish actions aimed at 
preserving currency stability from those 
intended to achieve domestic monetary 
objectives, with rates remaining a 
potential, though not immediate, tool in 
the defence framework.

However, global developments have 
altered that dynamic . Rising US yields 
and a gradual increase in the US Fed 
Funds rate (DBS baseline: a total of 75bp 
hikes) have compressed IN -US spreads. 
The recent sell -off in INR benchmark

### Page 6

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            6

bonds has effectively widened Gsec -
UST spreads across maturities, in 
contrast to the compression witnessed in 
regional high-yielders.

The latest announcements may not 
fully quell speculation over further 
policy action.

Upcoming measures might include -

-Markets anticipate relief on the long -
term capital gains tax structure on 
equities to draw in longer-duration 
inflows. This measure will boost inflows 
if it coincides with other factors that 
make domestic equities attractive for 
thematic investing , as global risk -free 
rates moderate.

-Street expectations also include steps 
to boost inward dollar repatriation by 
exporters to boost domestic dollar 
supply.

-Wider policy rate corridor akin to 2013.

-Policy rate response. While this might 
not be a panacea, it is a likely necessary 
condition. Raising the neutral rate might 
have mixed success in attracting capital 
market inflows ; though rate-sensitive 
flows are likely to get a fillip as investors 
seek attractive rate differentials in the 
region.

An adequate real rate cushion would 
also align with the evolving domestic 
inflation realities, which are expected to 
accelerate to 6% in the Oct-Dec quarter 
and remain above 5% well into 2027.

Given fluid global developments, we 
outline three scenarios on the rate path 
ahead. Our baseline assumption  is for 
75bp hikes in this cycle  (including Oct), 
spread between the Dece mber and 
February 2027 meetings. We don’t rule 
out an intermeeting rate hike before the 
December meeting, contingent on 
rupee movements.

Under the second scenario , s hould 
depreciation pressures on the rupee 
return, the 50bp hike might be brought 
forward to December , with another in 
February, taking cumulative hikes in this 
cycle to 100bp. The t hird scenario  is a 
more aggressive 150bp worth of hikes in 
this cycle, with 50bp front -loaded in 
December, and the rest spilling into 2027. 
Factors that  will determine whether 
these scenarios materialise are the 
resumption and scale of rupee 
depreciation, oil price direction, US Fed 
Funds rate increases (DBSf: total 75bps) 
and other risk-off events.

Path ahead

Despite these measures, we see limited 
room for sustained INR appreciation on 
grounds of manufacturing/ trade 
competitiveness. A challenging flows 
portfolio flows outlook leaves the d oor 
open to further gradual rupee 
depreciation subject to global 
developments, with high sensitivity to oil 
price movements and risk sentiments . A 
strong rate response will help introduce  
favourable rate differentials and reduce 
currency volatility.

### Page 7

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            7

Group Research 
ECONOMICS & STRATEGY

Taimur BAIG, Ph.D. 
Chief Economist 
Global 
taimurbaig@dbs.com

## Document 390: Lumentum Q1营收同比飙升136%，斩获50亿美元订单承诺，Q2指引大幅超预期 _ 财报见闻

Source PDF: all report pdf/Lumentum Q1营收同比飙升136%，斩获50亿美元订单承诺，Q2指引大幅超预期 _ 财报见闻.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Lumentum 2027 财年 Q1 营收同⽐增⻓ 136.5% ⾄ 12.63 亿美元，调整后每股收益 4.57 美
元，均超预期。受 AI 光互联需求推动，公司斩获第⼆个 NPO/CPO 重⼤项⽬，采购承诺超
50 亿美元。第⼆季度净营收指引为 15.75 亿⾄ 16.25 亿美元，远超市场预估的 14.8 亿美元。
摘要：
光⼦技术公司 Lumentum 在 AI 基础设施投资浪潮中收获丰厚，交出⼀份全⾯超预期的季度成绩
单，并以强劲的前瞻指引向市场传递出需求加速的信号。
Lumentum 公布 2027 财年第⼀季度（截⾄ 2026 年 9 ⽉ 26 ⽇）业绩，净营收 12.63 亿美元，同⽐⼤
增 136.5% ；调整后每股收益 4.57 美元，⾼于市场预估的 4.21 美元；调整后⽑利率 53.2% ，超出
预估的 51.3% 。
与此同时，公司第⼆季度净营收指引为 15.75 亿⾄ 16.25 亿美元，远超市场预估的 14.8 亿美元，
调整后每股收益指引 5.90 ⾄ 6.20 美元，亦⼤幅优于预估的 5.07 美元。

在业绩亮点之外， Lumentum 总裁兼⾸席执⾏官 Michael Hurlston 披露，公司于本季度斩获第⼆
个重⼤近封装光学 / 共封装光学（ NPO/CPO ）项⽬订单，背后附有逾 50 亿美元的采购承诺。他
同时表⽰，光学电路交换（ OCS ）的需求能⻅度近⼏个⽉已显著扩⼤，有望成⻓为年营收规模
达数⼗亿美元的业务板块。 Lumentum 美股盘前涨 2.46% 。
营收与利润率全⾯突破
Lumentum 第⼀财季净营收 12.63 亿美元，环⽐增⻓ 25.5% ，同⽐增⻓ 136.5% 。上⼀财年同期营
收仅为 5.34 亿美元，可⻅公司规模在⼀年内已实现翻倍有余。
盈利能⼒同步⼤幅改善。 GAAP ⽑利率从⼀年前的 34.0% 提升⾄ 51.0% ，⾮ GAAP ⽑利率则达到
53.2% ，同⽐扩张约 1380 个基点。⾮ GAAP 营业利润率为 42.3% ，较去年同期的 18.7% ⼤幅跃升
2360 个基点；⾮ GAAP 营业利润为 5.344 亿美元，超出分析师预估的 5.007 亿美元。

按产品类型划分，系统业务表现尤为突出，本季度营收 5.084 亿美元，同⽐增⻓ 228.8% ，环⽐增
⻓ 42.4% ；组件业务营收 7.542 亿美元，同⽐增⻓ 98.9% ，环⽐增⻓ 16.1% 。系统业务的⾼速增
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

⻛险提⽰及免责条款

⻓折射出 OCS 等⾼附加值产品需求的强劲爆发。
NPO/CPO ⼤单落地， OCS 驶⼊多⼗亿级赛道
本季度最具战略意义的进展，来⾃ NPO/CPO 领域的⼜⼀重⼤订单突破。 Michael Hurlston 表
⽰，公司取得了 " 第⼆个重⼤ NPO/CPO 项⽬胜利 " ，并获得超过 50 亿美元的采购承诺背书。这⼀
⻓期订单承诺不仅为公司未来营收提供了可⻅度⽀撑，也验证了 Lumentum 在 AI 数据中⼼光互联
领域的竞争地位。
OCS ⽅⾯， Hurlston 指出，过去⼏个⽉内需求能⻅度已 " 显著扩⼤ " ，公司预计光学交换业务将发
展成为 " 年营收规模达数⼗亿美元的业务流 " 。泵浦激光器与 OCS 是本季度增⻓的主要驱动⼒，
云模块业务亦呈现⽑利率改善的积极趋势。

Q2 指引⼤幅超预期，单季增量堪⽐两年前全季营收
展望第⼆财季，Lumentum 预计净营收区间为 15.75 亿⾄ 16.25 亿美元，⾮ GAAP 营业利润率为
45.0% ⾄ 46.0% ，⾮ GAAP 摊薄每股收益为 5.90 ⾄ 6.20 美元，三项指标均全⾯超出市场预期。
Hurlston 对此以更直观的⽅式加以描绘： " 我们预计第⼆季度将实现约 3.5 亿美元的环⽐增⻓，这
⼀数字已超过两年前公司整个季度的营收规模。 " 他还强调，第⼆季度营业利润率预计将突破
45% 。
从绝对数字来看，若以第⼆季度指引中值 16 亿美元计算，公司单季营收将较⼀年前增⻓约两
倍，增⻓斜率依然陡峭。
现⾦流动与资产负债情况
截⾄ 2026 年 9 ⽉ 26 ⽇， Lumentum 持有现⾦、现⾦等价物及短期投资合计 20.0 亿美元，较上季度
末减少 7.588 亿美元。流动资产端，应收账款增⾄ 6.257 亿美元，存货增⾄ 8.077 亿美元，反映出
业务规模快速扩张对营运资本的消耗。
资本⽀出⽅⾯，本季度固定资产净值增⾄ 14.104 亿美元，较上季度末的 11.591 亿美元明显提

升，北卡罗来纳州格林斯伯勒⽣产基地的产能爬坡仍在持续推进。公司调整后 EBITDA 为 5.78 亿
美元，较上年同期的 1.276 亿美元⼤幅提升。

Westpac New Zealand Economics
Turning point?
• We expect the unemployment rate to edge
down to 5.5% for the September quarter.
• Job numbers have been gaining momentum
in recent months, and now look to be enough
to outstrip population growth. However, the
participation rate will be the deciding factor.
• Despite cost-of-living pressures on workers,
the existing slack in the labour market is likely
to keep wage growth subdued.
• Our forecasts are a little more positive than

what the RBNZ assumed in their September
policy statement. Assuming they deliver on
an OCR hike this afternoon, the labour market
surveys could have an important bearing on
whether they follow up with another hike in
December.

The New Zealand economy has returned to growth over
the last year, despite a number of headwinds. That’s been
accompanied by some modest growth in jobs, though
to date it hasn’t been enough to prevent a further rise

in the unemployment rate. However, we think that next
Wednesday’s labour market reports could show the jobs
market reaching a turning point.
We expect the unemployment rate to ease from 5.6%
to 5.5% in the September quarter, which would mark
the first fall in almost five years. That’s not a firmly held
view, given the usual margin of uncertainty around this
ECONOMIC BULLETIN

28 Oct 2026 | Michael Gordon, Senior Economist | +64 9 336 5670 | michael.gordon@westpac.co.nz

### Page 2

2
Westpac New Zealand Economics
measure. But we’re now seeing consistent evidence
that jobs growth is outstripping the modest pace of
population growth. We also note that the previous result
was 5.56% in unrounded terms, so it wouldn’t take much
to tip the rounded rate down to 5.5% this time.
On the whole, our forecasts are a little stronger than
what the RBNZ assumed in its September Monetary
Policy Statement. While our unemployment rate forecast

is identical, we’re more positive on both employment
and labour force participation. Meanwhile, our pick of a
0.5% rise in the Labour Cost Index is slightly ahead of the
RBNZ’s estimate of 0.4%.
Assuming that the RBNZ delivers the OCR hike that we
expect this afternoon, we think they will be noncommittal
about the chances of another hike at the 9 December
Monetary Policy Statement. In that respect, the labour
market surveys could be important in setting market

expectations for the December review, as there aren’t
that many major data releases between now and then.
Forecast details.
We expect the Household Labour Force Survey (HLFS)
to show a 0.5% rise in employment for the September
quarter. That’s broadly in line with the Monthly
Employment Indicator (MEI), with the September release
this morning showing a third straight month of growth
in filled jobs. We note that employment growth in the
HLFS has actually outstripped the MEI for the last nine

quarters, but it’s not clear why this has been the case and
we’re wary of extrapolating it too far into the next quarter.

Employment growth of around 0.5% would be more than
enough to absorb the modest 0.3% rise in the working-
age population, and on its own would suggest a fall in

the unemployment rate. But it also comes down to the
rate of labour force participation, which we don’t really
have much guidance on. The participation rate has been
choppy over recent quarters, and in the June quarter
we saw an unusually large 0.3pt rise to 70.7%; we’re
assuming some payback this time with a more modest
0.1pt rise to 70.8%.
We’ll also be keeping an eye on the broader indicators of
slack in the labour market, although we don’t formally use

these in our forecasts. The June quarter saw a sharp rise
in the underutilisation rate from 12.9% to 13.8%. However,
we think there’s some residual seasonality in this series,
relating to one component in particular: people who are
available for work but not actively looking. In that case,
we could see a substantial pullback in the underutilisation
measure this time.
Previously we’ve characterised the labour market as a
“low-fire, low-hire” environment, with much of the rise in

unemployment reflecting the difficulty of breaking into
the workforce, rather than job losses. There are some
signs of a thaw emerging, with job vacancies picking up
from their lows over the last year and business surveys
pointing to a lift in hiring intentions. But we still expect
that it will be a long road to recovery – our current
forecasts see the unemployment rate at 5% by the end of
next year.
While cost-of-living pressures are again rearing their

head as a major concern for workers, the existing degree
of slack in the labour market will continue to make it
difficult for them to push for bigger pay increases. We
expect a 0.5% rise in the Labour Cost Index (LCI) for
the September quarter, which would nudge the annual
growth rate up slightly to 2.1%. That said, we don’t have a
lot to go on for this forecast. The MEI includes a measure
of median weekly earnings, which has shown no sign of
acceleration so far – but this is only a very loose indicator

of wage pressures, as it will also reflect any changes in
the composition of jobs and pay rates.

CENTRAL BANK MEETINGS

People Bank of China (October 20)

We expect the PBOC to keep the 1Y LPR 
unchanged next week . Industrial 
production is expected to edge up from 
5.2% yoy in August to 5.3% in September, 
supported by firm external demand. 
Exports stayed strong, rising 36.3% yoy 
in September, driven by the regional AI -
electronics upcycle.

Domestic momentum, however, remains 
uneven. Retail sales growth is expected 
to moderate to 0.3% yoy  in September. 
Household sentiment is weak amid 
uncertain job prospects, slower income 
growth and elevated precautionary 
savings, while subdued property prices 
continue to weigh on household wealth 
and consumption. Investment also stays 
weak, with fixed as set investment 
expected to contract further by 7.5% yoy 
ytd in September. Corporate investment 
is cautious amid the anti -involution 
campaign and geopolitical tensions, 
while the property sector continues to be 
a major drag.

Policymakers are likely to maintain a 
measured policy stance, relying on 
targeted fiscal support to sustain growth 
rather than further rate cuts.

Bank Indonesia and Bangko Sentral ng 
Pilipinas (Oct 21 and 22).

Bank Indonesia is expected to extend its 
pause, as they lean on non-rate measures, 
including intervention and hedging 
facilities to help the currency.  On Friday, 
MSCI signaled a  potential shift in 
Indonesia’s status to a standalone 
status, rather than a reclassification to 
frontier market , after consultations are 
complete in mid -2027. Any ensuing risk -
off move in the currency , would likely 
prompt greater central bank vigilance 
and intervention.

Our base case for the benchmark rate to 
be held unchanged this quarter , backed 
by a lower conviction around back -to-
back US rate hikes in Oct and relative 
rupiah stability. Separately, the 
government announced additional 
support for low-income households in 4Q 
by way of in -kind assistance and wage 
subsidies. This year’s fiscal deficit i s 
likely to close in at -2.85% of GDP, close 
to our forecast at -2.9%, but few 
uncertainties facing the 2027 math (note).

We expect a 25bp hike from the BSP to 
5.25% in October , after three rate 
increases earlier in the year. Rising by the 
highest pace in Asia, Philippines inflation 
surged to 7.2% y/y in September from 6.1% 
in August, matching this year's peak and 
approaching a three -year high.  Inflation 
risks remain tilted to the upside, driven by 
further public transport fare adjustments, 
elevated oil prices, recent wage hikes 
and peso depreciation. Fiscal space is, 
meanwhile, limited to support 
households, via subsidies and/ instate 
price caps. Governor Remolona also 
signalled that the BSP remains ready to 
act on rates to contain inflation. With 
inflationary pressures likely to be 
persistent, the likelihood of further hikes 
in 4Q26 rises.

### Page 3

The Week Ahead October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                         3

Bank of Korea (October 22)

The Bank of Korea is expected to hold its 
policy rate at 3.00%, following back -to-
back rate hikes in July and August. AI -
driven growth momentum remains strong, 
as reflected in September export growth 
of around 80% YoY. However, the 
urgency for further tightening has eased, 
with CPI inflation stabilising at 2.9% YoY 
in September. The recent KOSPI 
correction and continued KRW strength 
also reduce the need for another near -
term hike. We maintain our call for a final 
25bp rate hike in November, bringing the 
policy rate to 3.25% by year-end.

FORTHCOMING DATA 
RELEASES

Japan

September trade and CPI data are due 
shortly. Export growth is expected to 
remain solid at 24% YoY, supported by 
continued strength in AI -related 
semiconductor exports. CPI inflation is 
projected to rise notably to 2.6% YoY, 
driven by the phase -out of publ ic utility 
subsidies, higher global energy prices, 
and persistent inflationary pressures 
from a weak yen. The BOJ is likely to 
increasingly acknowledge that its 2% 
inflation target is within reach. However, 
it is expected to keep rates unchanged 
at 1.25% at its October 29–30 meeting to 
assess the impact of the latest rate hike 
in September.

Taiwan

September export orders and industrial 
production data are due shortly. Export 
orders are expected to sustain 60% YoY 
growth, while industrial production is 
projected to expand by 20% YoY. The 
global AI boom faces growing headwinds 
from hyperscalers’ cash flow pressures, 
central bank rate hikes, and higher bond 
yields. However, our high -frequency 
indicators across AI demand (token 
expenditure), investment (hyperscaler  
capex and data centre construction 
spending), and supply (semiconductor 
sales and exports) show no clear signs of 
a significant slowdown so far.

Malaysia

Malaysia’s external demand likely 
remained strong in September 2026, 
while inflation edged up slightly. We 
expect export growth to sustain its 
robust expansion at 45% yoy , with 
momentum anchored by strong demand 
for electrical and electronics goods. Oil 
& gas shipments likely remained buoyant. 
However, high base effects prevented a 
faster pace of expansion.

We expect headline inflation to rise 
modestly to 2.1% yoy in September 2026. 
The renewed increase in global oil prices 
likely contributed to higher increases in 
domestic energy costs, although fuel 
subsidies continued to limit the cost 
pass-through and keep underlying 
inflationary pressures contained. Food 
inflation likely also firmed, with adverse 
weather conditions associated with El 
Nino posing upside risks.

### Page 4

The Week Ahead October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                         4

Singapore

We expect Singapore’s core and 
headline inflation to edge up slightly to 
2.3% yoy and 2.4% yoy , respectively, in 
September 2026, following modest 
increases to 2.2% yoy and 2.3% yoy in 
August. Inflationary pressures likely 
reflected firm private transport price 
increases amid higher petrol prices 
despite slower car price growth , the 
ongoing pass -through of higher global 
energy prices to fuel -exposed services 
items, positive contribution from 
elevated electricity & gas prices, firmer 
food inflation, and a slight increase in 
still-muted accommodation inflation. 
Firm, albeit not acc elerating, inflation in 
September 2026, which may be close to 
its peak in the Monetary Authority of 
Singapore’s assessment, likely validated 
its decision to maintain SGD NEER policy 
settings at the October 2026 review, 
following two proactive tightening 
moves in April and July.

Hong Kong

CPI is projected to edge up from 1.7% yoy 
in August to 1.9% in September, 
supported by improving consumption 
sentiment and higher global energy 
prices. Mainland visitor arrivals 
increased by 29.0% yoy in September on 
a daily average basis, providing furthe r 
support to retail spending and 
consumer-related services. Meanwhile, 
renewed conflict in the Middle East 
during the month has pushed up global 
energy prices, adding to upward 
pressure on utility and transport costs.

聯亞 3081 TT  ( 電子–通信網路 ) 
矽光閃爍、耀眼奪目 
投資與評價 
1. 聯亞 3Q26 因矽光應用持續擴張，獲利為歷史新高；4Q26 則受惠矽光
產品需求依舊強勁，獲利再創新高。 
2. 由於資料中心高階 800G 與 1.6T 產品出貨增加 ， 外加矽光所需後段與代
工產能持續開出，聯亞在美系資料中心客戶需求支撐，26 年與 27 年營
運續創新高，預估 26 年 EPS 21.49 元、27 年 EPS 65.65 元。 
3. 目前股價約 36.1X 2027E PER，位於近 3 年本益比參考區間 20~80X 中
間偏下位置，預期在 InP 基板需求穩定供應後，1H27 營運得以繼續走
高，維持買進投資評等，但考量2H26 營運成長幅度低於 1H26，故將股
價本益比評價由高於平均值之 55 倍下調至低於平均值之 45 倍位置，目
標價 3,000 元(45X 修正後 2027E PER)。 
重點更新 
矽光應用持續擴張，聯亞 3Q26 營運創高 
因美系資料中心客戶加速擴建 AI 資料中心，無論是聯亞與客戶共同開發，或
客戶直接與光通模組廠定購模組產品，對於 CW Laser 晶片或 EPI Wafer 需
求都相當強勁，且公司下游晶片製程代工廠家數亦持續增加，可望舒緩因晶
片製程產能不足的現況 ， 聯亞3Q26 整體營收為 15.62 億元(+27.9% QoQ)，
與預估之 15.54 億元( +27.2% QoQ)相近，毛利率上升至 58.6%，與預估之
58.5%相當 ， 營業費用增加至1.44 億元(+10.9% QoQ)， 但因營運規模擴大 ，
營業費用率仍較上季之 10.6%降至 9.2%， 營業淨利預估為7.71 億元(+34.7% 
QoQ)與預估之 7.75 億元(+35.4% QoQ)相近，稅後淨利 6.23 億元(+32.4% 
QoQ)，EPS 6.12 元，營運創新高。 
聯亞 4Q26 受惠矽光產品需求強勁，獲利再創新高 
聯亞 4Q26 受惠矽光產品需求強勁，客戶已下單半年期以上的長期定單，再
加上新訂購 E-beam 設備已投入量產，也增加台灣地區下游合作夥伴，也多
為相對較大的集團，資本支出承諾與投入資源較早期合作夥伴具規模性，除
此外公司已完成 200G 以上 PD 的驗證，規劃部份產能因應，隨產能增加將
開始量產 Data Center用 PD， 略微調高營收預估1.3%至 18.71 億元(+19.8% 
QoQ)， 毛利率預估也隨營運規模逐漸擴張而上調至59.1%， 營業淨利將季增
25.1%至 9.65 億元，稅後淨利預估為 7.76 億元(+24.6% QoQ)，EPS 7.62
元，獲利再創新高。

訪談內容 
矽光應用持續擴張，聯亞 3Q26 營運創高 
因美系資料中心客戶加速擴建 AI 資料中心，無論是聯亞與客戶共同開發，或客戶直接與光通模組廠訂
購模組產品，對於CW Laser 晶片或 EPI Wafer 需求都相當強勁，且公司下游晶片製程代工廠家數亦持
續增加，可望舒緩因晶片製程產能不足的現況，聯亞3Q26 整體營收為 15.62 億元(+27.9% QoQ)，與
預估之 15.54 億元( +27.2% QoQ)相近，毛利率上升至 58.6%，與預估之 58.5%相當，雖營業費用因
研發費用及隨獲利增加按比例提列的員工分紅費用增加至 1.44 億元(+10.9% QoQ) ，但由於營運規模
擴大，營業費用率仍較上季之10.6%降至 9.2%，營業淨利為 7.71 億元(+34.7% QoQ)，與預估之 7.75
億元(+35.4% QoQ)相近，稅後淨利 6.23 億元(+32.4% QoQ)，EPS 6.12 元，營運創新高。

聯亞 4Q26 受惠矽光產品需求強勁，獲利再創新高 
聯亞 4Q26 受惠矽光產品需求強勁，客戶已下單半年期以上的長期定單，再加上新訂購 E-beam 設備
已投入量產，也增加台灣地區下游合作夥伴，也多為相對較大的集團，資本支出承諾與投入資源較早期
合作夥伴具規模性，除此外公司已完成200G 以上 PD 的驗證，規劃部份產能因應，隨產能增加將開始
量產 Data Center 用 PD，略微調高營收預估 1.3%至 18.71 億元(+19.8% QoQ) ，毛利率預估也隨營
運規模逐漸擴張而上調至 59.1%，營業淨利將季增 25.1%至 9.65 億元，稅後淨利預估為 7.76 億元
(+24.6% QoQ)，EPS 7.62 元，獲利再創新高。

受惠更多客戶導入矽光產品，預期聯亞 26 年獲利將創歷史新高 
聯亞 26 年因中系與美系資料中心客戶持續在 800G 矽光產品增加導入，原先應用於 AI 功能產品開始
拓展至更多一般資料中心，將有更多客戶由400G 升級至 800G、甚至已開始導入 1.6T，公司持續以晶
圓或晶片方式出貨予模組廠商，帶動營收成長趨勢不變。原先擔憂因基板管制造成的生產排程調整影
響，4Q25 已逐漸恢復正常出貨節奏，目前已與兩家日本及一家美國基板供應商簽訂未來數年長期供貨
合約 ， 有助於穩固其InP 基板的供貨來源 ， 故略微上調全年營收預估0.6%至 55.59 億元(+152.3% YoY)，
毛利率預估維持至 57.9%，營業淨利可望年增 427.8%至 26.82 億元，稅後淨利預估為 21.88 億元
(+410.8% YoY)，以 10.18 億股本計算，EPS 21.49 元，獲利為歷史新高。 
預期聯亞 27 年受惠矽光需求強勁，營運再創新高 
受矽光產品成為資料中心短中距離光通訊收發器主流規格，其中所使用之CW Laser 需求持續攀升，市
場供應有限，且自 2Q26 後開始往 1.6T 產品投入，使用顆數增加或規格提升皆有助於聯亞在發射端晶
片的銷售與整體產品組合的改善 ， 且在2H26 將有更多後段產能逐漸投入 ， 也將增加前段大型MOCVD
機台的產能投入，下游封裝代工廠亦願意配合擴廠，InP 基板供貨分散於中系產地以外廠商，逐一洽談
與簽定長約，故調高 27 年整體營收預估 3.6%至 141.43 億元(+154.4% YoY)，毛利率走高至 62.0%，
即使上修營業費用預估 3.1%至 6.47 億元(+20.9% YoY)， 營業費用率仍由26 年之 9.6%下降至 4.6%，
故上調營業淨利預估 3.7%至 81.17 億元(+202.7% YoY)，稅後淨利 66.84 億元(+205.5% YoY)，EPS 
65.65 元，營運再創新高。

### Page 4

矽光需求推升聯亞 26 與 27 年營運續升 
聯亞 3Q26 因矽光應用持續擴張，營運符合預期且為歷史新高；預期 4Q26 受惠矽光產品需求依舊強
勁，營運將再創新高。由於資料中心高階 800G 與 1.6T 產品出貨增加，外加矽光所需後段與代工產能
持續開出，聯亞在美系資料中心客戶需求支撐，亦穩固其 InP 基板的供貨來源，26 年與 27 年營運續
創新高，預估 26 年 EPS 21.49 元、27 年 EPS 65.65 元。

目前股價約 36.1X 2027E PER，位於近 3 年本益比參考區間 20~80X 中間偏下位置，預期在 InP 基板
需求穩定供應後 ，1H27 營運得以繼續走高 ， 維持買進投資評等 ， 但考量2H26 營運成長幅度低於 1H26，
故將股價本益比評價由高於平均值之 55 倍下調至平均值之 45 倍位置，目標價由 3,500 元(55X 修正
後 2027E PER)調整至 3,000 元(45X 修正後 2027E PER)。

公司簡介 
聯亞光電(3081 TT)成立於 1997 年 ， 以有機金屬氣相磊晶(MOCVD)技術生產 LD(雷射)磊晶片與 PD(光
檢測器)磊晶片，為光通訊產業上游關鍵元件雷射磊晶片供應商，產品包含：磷化銦 (InP)雷射磊晶片、
砷化鎵(GaAs)雷射磊晶片及光檢測器(PD)磊晶片 。 舉凡行動通訊網路之4G 與 5G 基地台建置 、FTTX、
雲端運算、數據中心等需求，均會用到該公司的產品。 
光通訊用雷射材料特性複雜、相關生產參數設定困難，技術門檻非常高，新進公司熬4、5 年虧損期，
也不見得能掌握到雷射磊晶技術，導致全球能提供雷射磊晶片公司不到10 家。而聯亞技術領導團隊曾
在美國貝爾實驗室與中華電信研究所從事雷射研究 ， 擁有深厚的III-V 族雷射磊晶技術並掌握材料特性 ，
除滿足客戶少量多樣需求外，亦能提供客戶完整技術諮詢與協助，合作開發新產品。 
3Q26 以應用市場分類營收占比：Telecom 相關產品 5~10%、Datacom 80~85%(多數為矽光產品)、
其他 5%。

### Page 5

聯亞 ESG 政策 
1. 溫室氣體管理：聯亞光電自 2022 年起根據 ISO 14064 -1 進行溫室氣體盤查，以確實掌握各廠區溫室氣體排放
源及排放量，並依據盤查結果推動各項節能減排措施，透過持續改善之計劃，降低本公司因溫室氣體排放對地球暖
化所造成之環境及氣候衝擊，善盡企業社會責任。2023 年度溫室氣體總排放量相較於 2022 年減少 14%，主因為
營收規模下滑磊晶片產出量大幅降低所致，故 2023 年度單位產品溫室氣體排放量較 2022 年度增加 147%。 
2. 水資源管理：近年因全球氣候暖化的結果，造成缺水與水災的風險日益升高，為降低水資源不穩定對營運活動造
成的衝擊，聯亞光電以 ISO14001 的管理體系為基礎進行水資源管理，持續推動水資源回收再利用及節約措施。本
公司由自來水廠取得用水，冰機冷卻水塔運轉所需水量及蒸發量；製程產生之廢氣，洗滌時所產生的蒸發量。製程
端在相同用水量的情況下提高產值，增加用水效率；廠區及建築設置水表，每日抄表進行水量控管。近三年度節水
方案與成效為聯亞光電投入多項節水改善方案，包括冷卻水塔濃縮倍率提升、空調箱冷盤管冷凝水回收、純水 RO-
R 回收，2023 年在水資源運用管理及節水技術執行下，約有 13.65 百萬公升節水量，節水比例約為 28.05%。 
3. 廢棄物管理：公司主要廢棄物包含製程廢水處理程序所產生之汙泥、製程所使用之化學品廢液、沾染化學品之無
塵布及濾材、廢棄晶片；另有員工生活垃圾及非製程產生之包材、廢塑膠等廢棄物，皆委由合格之清除處理廠商進
行處理。短期目標為 2025 年每單位產品產出廢棄物較 2020 年降低 10%，中長期目標為 2030 年每單位產品產出
廢棄物較 2020 年降低 20%。 
4. 能源管理：電力使用為聯亞光電在能源消耗的主要部分，其餘包含少部分石化燃料使用 ( 汽油、柴油等 )。本公
司持續推動各項節能改善專案，除了落實節電措施、提高能源效率，亦同時推廣落實辦公室及公共區域節能管理方
案，輔以相關之宣導活動，增進同仁節能減碳之觀念與習慣。落實能源管理法訂定之節約能源目標及計畫，達成年
度節電率，現有設備調整優化。導入ISO 50001 能源管理系統認證，對於再生能源使用比率， 2030 年目標：非生
產廠房 100％使用再生能源；生產廠房 20％用電量為再生。

《高利高通膨壓抑，留意日股短線震盪加大》
10月日本股市大幅震盪修正 ，符合本行下調評等之
預期。其中，日經225指數在10月初一度上破7萬點
大館，此後因AI相關估值疑慮、科技股修正與長端利
率上行，全球科技股估值大幅修正，日經225指數在
10月23日盤中一度下探61,462點。
日本兩大指數在本月表現也呈現相對分化。日經225
權重權重集中於東京威力科創、愛德萬測試、軟銀集
團等半導體與 AI標的，在本波修正幅度較大；相反
的，TOPIX指數因金融、內需與防禦類股權重較高，
加上利率下行有利銀行以外的內需類股，回檔相對溫
和。也間接反映了10月的修正偏向是 「AI交易擁擠
的釋放」，而非全面性的景氣衰退定價。
債市方面，10年期日本公債殖利率在10月初一度升

至3.11%、創1999年以來新高 ，反映日本央行升
息、以及高市內閣財政擴張的雙重疑慮。
此後殖利率一路回落至 10月26日的2.82%、下行約
23個基點。這一下行幅度反映全球 AI相關信用風險
引發的避險需求。
重點摘要
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026/9，中國信託商業銀行整理
5
AI相關估值疑慮、科技股修正
• 10月日股受AI估值修正及長天
期利率波動影響而震盪下跌，
但修正主要集中於半導體與AI
權值股。
• TOPIX相對抗跌，顯示市場仍
未全面反映景氣衰退，而是資
金自擁擠交易部位撤出。

《日本出口維持韌性，內需動能出現疑慮》
經濟基本面部分，日本9月出口在半導體與AI相關需
求帶動下，連續13個月維持正成長 ，惟增速放緩至
16.7%、略低於預期；但進口年增卻升至18.9%，主
要因為進口能源價格大漲所致，導致貿易逆差擴大，
外需雖強卻未能轉化為淨出口貢獻。
內需與生產端則收高利高通膨壓力同步降溫。工業生
產增速自年中高點回落，製造業PMI降至七個月來最
低，整體擴張速度已低於上半年水準，細項中新訂單
與出口訂單成長同步放緩，能源與勞動成本則大幅走
升，對企業營運形成壓力。內需改善亦相當有限，零
售銷售下滑、失業率微升、求人求職比回落，反映勞
動市場略有降溫；所幸，實質薪資已連續八個月正成
長、消費者信心小幅回升。

整體而言，日本經濟仍處於「名目成長、實質停滯」
的格局，擴張主要仰賴出口，內需復甦力道偏弱，而
高油價帶來的逆差壓力與生產動能轉弱，將是後續成
長的主要考驗 。基於地緣與油價的不確定性 ，10月
日本央行暫停升息，維持利率不變。
重點摘要
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026/10，中國信託商業銀行整理
5
貨幣政策正常化以及財政疑慮推升日債殖利率走高
• 日本出口持續受惠半導體與AI
需求，惟能源價格上漲推升進
口成本與貿易逆差。工業生產、
PMI及勞動市場同步降溫，經
濟仍維持「出口強、內需弱」
格局，成長動能面臨考驗。

《日銀暫停升息，12月升息預期支撐利率高檔》
日本央行已於 9月會期將政策利率上調 25個基點至
1.25%，為1995年以來最高 ，也是三個月內第二
次、1990年以來最快的升息節奏。
隨著近期經濟數據 ，10月份日央如預期暫停升息一
碼，會議同步公布《經濟與物價形勢展望報告》，根
據央行經濟預測顯示潛在通膨「已大致達到2%」。
配合植田和男在10月16日記者會曾表示，若基礎通
膨進一步上升將是 「無法接受」的，並在10月19日
國會答詢時表示潛在通膨非常接近2%目標。
加以隨著國際油價再度上破110美元大關，短期政策
重點從「對抗通縮 」轉為「防止通膨超標 」，是以
12月再度升息一碼的機率已大幅提高。
另一方，高市內閣的食品消費稅減稅與國防支出增

加，使市場對財源安排存疑 。不過，10月19日高市
在國會承諾在下一財年預算中妥善管控國債發行規
模，使近期長端利率有所回落 。但12月下一財年預
算與國債發行計畫公布時，財政紀律的可信度將再受
檢驗，預期短線利率上行趨緩，維持高檔盤整。
重點摘要
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026/10，中國信託商業銀行整理

底再升息機率。
• 另一方面，財政擴張與國債供
給疑慮仍在，預期長天期利率
維持高檔震盪。

不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行並不負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資之風險，並就投
資結果自行負責。未經本公司許可，本資料及訊息不得逕行抄錄、翻印或另作派發。本行以上市場資訊與分析，不涉及特定投資標的之建議。
《盤勢觀點-資金輪動加速，防禦成長族群接棒》
10月日本股市震盪 、類股表現分化 。市場資金明顯
由上半年漲幅龐大的AI硬體與半導體族群撤出，轉向
軟體服務、醫療保健及內需消費等防禦成長型產業。
其中，東京威力科創、SCREEN、瑞薩電子等指標股
普遍出現雙位數跌幅。相較之下，軟體服務類股大漲
17.9%，BayCurrent、SoftBank等個股表現突出。醫
藥生技、消費零售及娛樂觀光相關族群亦受惠於防禦
需求與內需消費韌性而表現相對亦強勢。值得注意的
是，銀行與非銀行金融股同步走弱，多家大型金融機
構跌幅介於8%至15%，顯示市場擔憂金融條件收緊
可能壓抑經濟活動與資本市場表現。
整體而言，10月日股市場風格由 「AI硬體主導」轉

向「防禦成長與內需消費主導」，同時對經濟基本面
疑慮小幅升溫 ，8~9月相對強勢的金融出現回調壓
力。展望未來，短期盤勢仍受利匯率壓力，以及科技
股評價調整影響 ，加以1H27預估日經225指數企業
獲利年增率轉為負成長，因此本行維持9月以來下調
日股至中性的看法不變，待國際油價回落以及年底年
度預算案底定後，可伺機布局。
重點摘要
5

禦成長族群。半導體、金融與
工業設備股表現疲弱，反映投
資人開始關注高利率環境及企
業獲利成長放緩風險。

不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行並不負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資之風險，並就投
資結果自行負責。未經本公司許可，本資料及訊息不得逕行抄錄、翻印或另作派發。本行以上市場資訊與分析，不涉及特定投資標的之建議。
《盤勢觀點-澳洲景氣放緩，股匯市轉向盤整》
隨著國際油價維持衝高，澳洲央行（RBA）於9月再
度升息將現金利率提高至 4.6%，官員雖表示若通膨
壓力持續，不排除進一步緊縮。但在高利率與高通膨
壓力持續積累下對景氣負面壓力逐漸加大。
資料顯示，澳洲9月失業率升至4.72%，已連續兩個
月 落 在 澳 央 主 席 認 為 可 有 效 壓 抑 通 膨 的 失 業 率
4.5~5.0%區間；而通膨方面 ，9月份澳洲 CPI雖由
4.0%升至4.3%，但核心CPI僅有2.2%，加以住房通
膨明顯回落，因此本行預期澳央轉向觀望、本輪升息
暫時告終，2027年政策轉向觀望。
外需方面，中國經濟基本面持續低迷，消費與投資不
振亦難以挹注澳洲出口動能，外需對澳幣支撐力量有

限，加以預期聯準會在 12月再度升息 ，將呈現「利
差有撐但優勢遞減」格局，因此預估澳幣短線維持在
0.7~0.71區間盤整。
綜上，持續升息後澳洲基本面轉弱，但高油價壓力又
迫使央行貨幣政策難以轉向寬鬆，加以企業獲利未見
改善動能，澳洲股市難以樂觀，本行維持中性展望。
重點摘要
• 高油價迫使澳洲央行維持偏
鷹態度 ，但高利率已逐步壓
抑就業與內需。
• 中國需求偏弱限制出口前景 ，
加上美澳利差優勢收斂 ，預
期澳幣短期維持區間震盪 ，
澳股展望偏中性。

保険業界の規制改革とインド経済
インド政府は、 過去四半世紀にわたり国民生活の安定と金融仲介機能の強化に向けて保険市場の自由化を
推進してきたが、ここにきて消費者保護強化に向けた規制強化にかじを切ろうとしている。
■規制緩
和を受けてインドの保険市場は急成長
インド政府は、国民生活の安定と金融仲介機
能の強化に向けて、保険市場の発展を推進して
きた。この背景としては、以下の2 点を指摘で
きる。
第 1 に、インドでは社会保障制度が整備途上
にある。そのため、予期せぬ疾病や事故をきっ
かけとする世帯所得の急減リスクを抑制するう
えで、医療保険や生命保険への加入が極めて重
要なセーフティネットとなる。
第 2 に、持続的なインド経済の発展のために
は、預入期間の短い銀行預金とは別に、インフ
ラ整備や産業育成の原動力となる長期かつ安定
的な国内資本の蓄積が必要である。保険会社が

集めて運用する巨額の資金は、まさにこの長期
資金供給の担い手として期待されてきた。
これらの目的の達成に向け、インド政府は過
去四半世紀にわたって段階的な規制緩和を進め
てきた（右上表）。2000年の民間企業や外資企
業の参入自由化を皮切りに、2002年のバンカシ
ュアランス（銀行窓口での保険販売）解禁、外
資出資比率上限の引き上げなどを進めた。
こうした一連の規制緩和によって多数の民
間・外国企業が参入し、 市場競争が活性化した。
日本からも日本生命や東京海上グループを含め、
複数の保険会社が参入し、対印投資を牽引した。
市場参入や販売に関わる規制緩和に加え、中
間所得層の拡大、家計における金融リテラシー
の向上、そして保険サービスのデジタル化も相
まって、インドの保険市場は飛躍的な拡大を遂
げた。保険料収入は経済成長率を上回るペースで拡大し、近年の保険会社の運用資産残高はGDP
の約 4分の 1に達している（右下図）。
インド政府は、独立 100 周年を迎える 2047 年までに全国民が自らのリスクに適した保険に手

ごろな価格でアクセスできる「Insurance for All by 2047」の実現を目標に掲げており、今後もイ
ンドの保険市場は基本的に底堅い成長をたどると見込まれる。
上席主任研究員 熊谷 章太郎
(kumagai.shotaro@jri.co.jp）

用）

2
■当局が規制強化に舵を切る理由
一方で、急激な市場拡大の副作用として、構造
的な問題も顕在化している。 最大の課題は保険販
売コストの高騰と不適切販売の横行である。 保険
販売競争が激化するなか、 保険会社が商業銀行や
ノンバンクといった販売代理店に対して支払う
手数料・報酬は、新規契約保険料を大幅に上回る
ペースで増加しており（右上図）、それが新規保
険料の上昇や将来の保険料支払いの減少につな
がることへの懸念が広がった。また、顧客のニー
ズに合致しない複雑な商品の強引な販売や、 商品
に関する説明不足も社会問題化した。
こうした状況を重くみた IRDAI（イン
ド保険規制開発局）は、市場の健全性を
維持すべく、足元で消費者保護を主眼と
した規制強化を検討している。2026 年 9
月に公表した改革案では、①販売代理店
への報酬構造の見直し、②保険会社の事
業費・管理費に対する規制厳格化、③販

売手法に対する規制厳格化、④コンプラ
イアンスの徹底に向けた流通チャネルの
合理化、⑤監査や情報公開制度の厳格化、
などの方向性が示された（右下表）。改
革案に関する意見を広く募ったうえで、
2027年前半に新たな制度を導入するとし
ている。
一連の規制強化の影響は、プラス面と
マイナス面に分けられる。まず、プラス
面としては、過度なインセンティブ競争
や不適切な販売が抑制されることで消費
者保護が大幅に強化され、保険市場の信
頼性と持続的発展の基盤が強化される点が挙げられる。
他方、マイナス面として懸念されるのは、移行期における副作用である。販売規制の厳格化に
伴い、保険会社および販売代理店である金融機関の収益拡大ペースの一時的な鈍化は避けられな
い。さらに、保険会社や金融機関がインドの金融市場における主たる投資主体であることを勘案
すると、規制強化が株式・債券価格に対して下押し圧力をもたらす公算が大きい。
保険市場のこうした変化は、今後のインド経済を展望するうえで重要な視点を提供する。すな

わち、保険業界に限らず多くの産業において、自由化による市場の急成長は様々な問題を生じさ
せ得るものの、そうした事態に対する規制の再設計・適正化こそが、新たな発展の礎になるとい
う点である。その嚆矢として保険をめぐる規制の行方が注目される。

ASIA MONTHLY  2026 年 11月（No.308）
